<div id="article-section" role="presentation">

<div class="AppleTopic apd-topic dark-mode-enabled book book-content">

<div>

<span id="iphbb8259c61"></span>

# Intro to Health data on iPhone

The Health app securely stores your health information from iPhone,
iPad, and Apple Watch, as well as from compatible third-party apps and
connected devices. You can also manually enter data for body
measurements, symptoms, menstrual cycle information, and more.

<div class="Alert">

**Siri:** On <a
href="https://support.apple.com/guide/iphone/aside/iph229706d23/18.0/ios/18.0"
class="xRef Aside">supported models</a>, say something like:
<span class="SaytoSiri">“What’s my heart rate?” or “How far have I
walked today?” </span>Not available in all languages or regions. <a
href="https://support.apple.com/guide/iphone/use-siri-iph83aad8922/18.0/ios/18.0"
class="xRef AppleTopic">Learn how to use Siri</a>.

</div>

<figure>
<img
src="https://help.apple.com/assets/6865787AA328F43F8508D109/68657882863ECDDE5707A431/en_US/8860e30ed17e3ac6dae5ff9c3d5c47e0.png"
data-originalimagename="Art/S2200_hlthHealthDetails_iPhone.png"
width="245" height="504"
alt="The Summary screen in Health. Information about activity, vitals, state of mind, and medications appears below Pinned." />
</figure>

<div id="ipha8741902a" class="Task">

## Health data from iPhone

<div id="aria-ipha8741902a" class="TaskBody" role="region"
aria-hidden="false">

iPhone automatically stores and analyzes valuable health data, including
the following:

- *Steps, flights of stairs, and other walking data:* Built-in sensors
  on your iPhone capture the number of steps you take, the number of
  flights of stairs you climb, your walking speed, and more. You can <a
  href="https://support.apple.com/guide/iphone/view-your-health-data-iphe3d379c32/18.0/ios/18.0"
  class="xRef AppleTopic">browse highlights, trends, and details</a>
  about this data in the Activity category in Health.

- *Mobility data:* iPhone stores important mobility metrics such as
  walking asymmetry, double support time, and more. Over time, as you
  walk with iPhone in your pocket or wear it near your waist, these
  metrics are used to calculate and record your walking steadiness. You
  can <a
  href="https://support.apple.com/guide/iphone/view-your-health-data-iphe3d379c32/18.0/ios/18.0"
  class="xRef AppleTopic">browse</a> this data in the Mobility category.
  You can also <a
  href="https://support.apple.com/guide/iphone/monitor-your-walking-steadiness-iphff1bf03ed/18.0/ios/18.0"
  class="xRef AppleTopic">receive a notification</a> and <a
  href="https://support.apple.com/guide/iphone/share-your-health-data-iph5ede58c3d/18.0/ios/18.0"
  class="xRef AppleTopic">share your notification</a> with others if
  your steadiness becomes low enough to put you at increased risk of
  falling in the next 12 months.

- *Headphone audio levels:* Audio level measurements from connected
  AirPods, EarPods, or other compatible headphones are automatically
  stored in Health. You can <a
  href="https://support.apple.com/guide/iphone/check-your-headphone-audio-levels-iph0596a9152/18.0/ios/18.0"
  class="xRef AppleTopic">review</a> this data in the Hearing category
  to help you understand your headphone listening habits.

- *Sleep history:* If you <a
  href="https://support.apple.com/guide/iphone/add-or-change-sleep-schedules-iph304a683a0/18.0/ios/18.0"
  class="xRef AppleTopic">set up a sleep schedule in Health</a>, iPhone
  estimates the periods you’re lying in bed with the intention to sleep.
  You can <a
  href="https://support.apple.com/guide/iphone/view-your-health-data-iphe3d379c32/18.0/ios/18.0"
  class="xRef AppleTopic">review this data</a> in the Sleep category to
  help you meet your sleep goals.

- *Health records from your healthcare providers:* You can <a
  href="https://support.apple.com/guide/iphone/download-health-records-iphc30019594/18.0/ios/18.0#iphe103f5a3e"
  class="xRef Task" data-"="">set up automatic downloads</a> of health
  records of your allergies, medical conditions, medications, and more
  from supported healthcare organizations (not available in all
  countries or regions). You can <a
  href="https://support.apple.com/guide/iphone/view-health-records-iph2b3a37ddd/18.0/ios/18.0"
  class="xRef AppleTopic">browse these records</a> in Health.

</div>

</div>

<div id="iph9076f32d6" class="Task">

## Health data from Apple Watch

<div id="aria-iph9076f32d6" class="TaskBody" role="region"
aria-hidden="false">

After you <a
href="https://support.apple.com/guide/iphone/apple-watch-iph9252a427b/18.0/ios/18.0"
class="xRef AppleTopic">pair iPhone</a> with Apple Watch, periodic heart
rate and blood oxygen measurements are automatically sent from
Apple Watch to Health, where you can <a
href="https://support.apple.com/guide/iphone/view-your-health-data-iphe3d379c32/18.0/ios/18.0#iph59b9b077f"
class="xRef Task" data-"="">browse highlights, trends, and details</a>
in the Heart and Respiratory categories. You can also set up Apple Watch
to send additional information to Health, including the following:

- *Exercise minutes and other activity metrics:* You can <a
  href="https://support.apple.com/guide/iphone/view-your-health-data-iphe3d379c32/18.0/ios/18.0#iph59b9b077f"
  class="xRef Task" data-"="">view these metrics</a> in the Activity
  category in Health on iPhone.

- *A history of time in daylight (Apple Watch SE 2nd generation, Apple
  Watch Series 6 or later, and all Apple Watch Ultra models):* The
  ambient light sensor in Apple Watch estimates how much time you’ve
  spent in daylight. You can <a
  href="https://support.apple.com/guide/iphone/view-your-health-data-iphe3d379c32/18.0/ios/18.0#iph59b9b077f"
  class="xRef Task" data-"="">view details</a> in the Other Data
  category in Health on iPhone.

- *Notifications about high or low heart rates, low cardio fitness
  (watchOS 7.2 or later), and irregular heart rhythms (not available in
  all countries or regions):* You can <a
  href="https://support.apple.com/guide/iphone/view-your-health-data-iphe3d379c32/18.0/ios/18.0#iph59b9b077f"
  class="xRef Task" data-"="">view the details</a> about these
  notifications in the Heart category.

- *Environmental noise levels and notifications about loud sounds:* You
  can <a
  href="https://support.apple.com/guide/iphone/view-your-health-data-iphe3d379c32/18.0/ios/18.0#iph59b9b077f"
  class="xRef Task" data-"="">view the details</a> about noise in your
  environment in the Hearing category.

- *Overnight health metrics in the Vitals app:* You can view overnight
  health metrics—heart rate, respiratory rate, wrist temperature, blood
  oxygen, and sleep duration—to get a better understanding of your daily
  health status. If you wear your Apple Watch to sleep for multiple
  nights, the Vitals app establishes a typical range for each of your
  health metrics. If multiple metrics are outside your typical range,
  you’ll receive a notification along with context for factors that may
  be involved—like medications or illness, for example. You can <a
  href="https://support.apple.com/guide/iphone/view-your-health-data-iphe3d379c32/18.0/ios/18.0#iph59b9b077f"
  class="xRef Task" data-"="">view the details</a> about your overnight
  vitals in the Vitals category.

- *A history of hard falls (Apple Watch SE and Apple Watch Series 4 and
  later):* In the Other Data category, you can <a
  href="https://support.apple.com/guide/iphone/view-your-health-data-iphe3d379c32/18.0/ios/18.0#iph59b9b077f"
  class="xRef Task" data-"="">view details</a> about the number of times
  fallen.

To learn more about health data from Apple Watch, see
<a href="https://support.apple.com/guide/watch/apd7941a2f19"
class="URL">Get started with health features</a>,
<a href="https://support.apple.com/guide/watch/apd3ab22534c"
class="URL">Time spent in daylight</a>,
<a href="https://support.apple.com/guide/watch/apd00a43a9cb"
class="URL">Measure noise levels</a>,
<a href="https://support.apple.com/guide/watch/apd15aa7ed96"
class="URL">Track your vitals</a>, and
<a href="https://support.apple.com/guide/watch/apd34c409704"
class="URL">Manage Fall Detection</a> in the Apple Watch User Guide.

</div>

</div>

<div id="iphfbfc1e6a0" class="Task">

## Health data from other apps and devices

<div id="aria-iphfbfc1e6a0" class="TaskBody" role="region"
aria-hidden="false">

As you set up health and fitness apps that you download from the
App Store, follow their onscreen instructions to allow them to share
data with Health.

To store data from devices like blood pressure monitors and weight
scales, follow their setup instructions. Bluetooth® devices <a
href="https://support.apple.com/guide/iphone/connect-bluetooth-accessories-iph3c50f191/18.0/ios/18.0#iph386380112"
class="xRef Task" data-"="">need to be paired</a> with iPhone.

<div class="Alert">

*Note:* You can <a
href="https://support.apple.com/guide/iphone/share-your-health-data-iph5ede58c3d/18.0/ios/18.0#iph87bdf560d"
class="xRef Task" data-"="">change health data permissions</a> for apps
and devices at any time.

</div>

</div>

</div>

<div id="iph6ccc2ded1" class="Task">

## Data that you add manually

<div id="aria-iph6ccc2ded1" class="TaskBody" role="region"
aria-hidden="false">

You can manually enter data about your menstrual cycles, medications,
body measurements, and more.

- *Add information about your menstrual cycles:* By logging menstrual
  cycle information, you can get period and fertile window predictions.
  See <a
  href="https://support.apple.com/guide/iphone/log-menstrual-cycle-information-iph51a822b18/18.0/ios/18.0"
  class="xRef AppleTopic">Log menstrual cycle information in Health</a>.

- *Add your medications, vitamins, and supplements:* By adding these
  items, you can get reminders to take them and learn more about them.
  See <a
  href="https://support.apple.com/guide/iphone/track-your-medications-iph811670c81/18.0/ios/18.0"
  class="xRef AppleTopic">Track your medications in Health</a>.

- *Set up a sleep schedule:* Scheduling regular times for getting to bed
  and waking up can help you meet your sleep goals. See <a
  href="https://support.apple.com/guide/iphone/set-up-a-schedule-for-a-sleep-focus-iphaf56dceb4/18.0/ios/18.0"
  class="xRef AppleTopic">Set up a Sleep Focus schedule in Health</a>.

- *Add information to a Medical ID:* You can provide information about
  your medical conditions, medications, emergency contacts, and more in
  an emergency Medical ID. First responders and others can view this
  critical information directly on your iPhone, even while it’s locked.
  See <a
  href="https://support.apple.com/guide/iphone/set-up-and-view-your-medical-id-iph08022b192/18.0/ios/18.0"
  class="xRef AppleTopic">Set up and view your Medical ID</a>.

- *Add information to other Health categories:* Tap Browse at the bottom
  right of Health, tap a category, tap <img
  src="https://help.apple.com/assets/6865787AA328F43F8508D109/68657882863ECDDE5707A431/en_US/465382cdc77d9a7ae9a6a530f3a2e55d.png"
  data-originalimagename="GlobalArt/IL_forwardButton.png" width="20"
  height="30" alt="the Details button" /> for the data you want to
  update, then tap Add Data at the top right.

</div>

</div>

<div class="Alert">

**Important:** iPhone, iPad, and Apple Watch aren’t medical devices. See
<a
href="https://support.apple.com/guide/iphone/important-safety-information-iph301fc905/18.0/ios/18.0"
class="xRef AppleTopic">Important safety information for iPhone</a>.

</div>

<div class="LinkUniversal">

**See also**<a
href="https://support.apple.com/guide/iphone/view-your-health-data-iphe3d379c32/18.0/ios/18.0"
class="xRef AppleTopic">View your data in Health on iPhone</a><a
href="https://support.apple.com/guide/iphone/share-your-health-data-iph5ede58c3d/18.0/ios/18.0"
class="xRef AppleTopic">Share your data in Health on iPhone</a>

</div>

</div>

</div>

</div>

</div>
